- `val_natAdd` (theorem, :338) — A gate wire carries the gate applied to the wires it names.  
  `@[simp] theorem val_natAdd (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) : D.val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = (D.gate i).eval fun a => D.val x (lift i a) `
- `val_gate` (theorem, :349) — `val_natAdd` under the name the W951 files use.  
  `theorem val_gate (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) : D.val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = (D.gate i).eval fun a => D.val x (lift i a) `
- `wire_cases` (theorem, :354) — Every wire is an input wire or a gate wire.  
  `theorem wire_cases {P : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (hin : ∀ j : Fin n, P (Fin.castAdd s j)) (hg : ∀ i : Fin s, P (Fin.natAdd n i)) : ∀ w, P w `
- `wire_induction` (theorem, :364) — **Wire induction.** To prove `P` for every wire: prove it for the inputs, and for gate `i` from all the wires gate `i` can name.  
  `theorem wire_induction {P : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (hin : ∀ j : Fin n, P (Fin.castAdd s j)) (hg : ∀ i : Fin s, (∀ a : Fin (n + i), P (lift i a)) → P (Fin.natAdd n i)) : ∀ w, P w `
- `valN` (def, :378) — The value at a natural-number wire index, `false` past the end (the W951 convention).  
  `def valN (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) (i : ℕ) : Bool `
- `valN_of_lt` (theorem, :381)  
  `theorem valN_of_lt (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) {i : ℕ} (h : i < n + s) : D.valN x i = D.val x ⟨i, h⟩ `
- `valN_of_ge` (theorem, :384)  
  `theorem valN_of_ge (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) {i : ℕ} (h : n + s ≤ i) : D.valN x i = false `
- `valN_val` (theorem, :387)  
  `@[simp] theorem valN_val (D : Dag n s) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) : D.valN x w.1 = D.val x w `
- `val_rel` (theorem, :398) — **Relational transfer.** Two dags of the same shape, two inputs, a relation `R` on values and a set `G` of wires.  
  `theorem val_rel (D D' : Dag n s) (x y : Fin n → Bool) (R : Bool → Bool → Prop) (G : Fin (n + s) → Prop) (hin : ∀ j : Fin n, G (Fin.castAdd s j) → R (x j) (y j)) (hg : ∀ i : Fin s, G (Fin.natAdd n i) → ∀ v v' : Fin (n + i`
- `agree_off` (theorem, :411) — **Agreement off a bad set.** If the two inputs agree outside `Bad` and every gate outside `Bad` reads only wires outside `Bad`, then every wire outside `Bad` ha  
  `theorem agree_off (D : Dag n s) (x y : Fin n → Bool) (Bad : Fin (n + s) → Prop) (hin : ∀ j : Fin n, ¬ Bad (Fin.castAdd s j) → x j = y j) (hg : ∀ i : Fin s, ¬ Bad (Fin.natAdd n i) → ∀ a ∈ (D.gate i).reads, ¬ Bad (lift i a`
- `agree_off₂` (theorem, :419) — Two-dag form of `agree_off`: additionally the two dags have the same gates outside `Bad`.  
  `theorem agree_off₂ (D D' : Dag n s) (x y : Fin n → Bool) (Bad : Fin (n + s) → Prop) (hin : ∀ j : Fin n, ¬ Bad (Fin.castAdd s j) → x j = y j) (hg : ∀ i : Fin s, ¬ Bad (Fin.natAdd n i) → D.gate i = D'.gate i ∧ ∀ a ∈ (D.gat`
- `NotFree` (def, :429) — A dag with no NOT gate.  
  `def NotFree (D : Dag n s) : Prop `
- `Mono` (def, :432) — A dag all of whose gates have a monotone operation (AND, OR, MAJ; any fan-in).  
  `def Mono (D : Dag n s) : Prop `
- `MonoOffNot` (def, :435) — A dag whose gates are NOT gates or monotone gates (the hypothesis of the sandwich).  
  `def MonoOffNot (D : Dag n s) : Prop `
- `Mono.monoOffNot` (theorem, :437)  
  `theorem Mono.monoOffNot {D : Dag n s} (hD : D.Mono) : D.MonoOffNot `
- `mono_of_and_or` (theorem, :440) — AND/OR dags are monotone dags.  
  `theorem mono_of_and_or (D : Dag n s) (h : ∀ i, (D.gate i).op = GateOp.and ∨ (D.gate i).op = GateOp.or) : D.Mono `
- `val_mono` (theorem, :445) — **Monotonicity** of dags with monotone gates.  
  `theorem val_mono (D : Dag n s) (hD : D.Mono) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : x ≤ y) (w : Fin (n + s)) : D.val x w ≤ D.val y w `
- `val_le_val` (theorem, :451) — Gatewise domination gives wirewise domination (used for the sandwich).  
  `theorem val_le_val (D D' : Dag n s) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : x ≤ y) (hg : ∀ (i : Fin s) (v v' : Fin (n + i) → Bool), v ≤ v' → (D.gate i).eval v ≤ (D'.gate i).eval v') (w : Fin (n + s)) : D.val x w ≤ D'.val y w `
- `take` (def, :460) — The first `k` gates.  
  `def take (D : Dag n s) (k : ℕ) (hk : k ≤ s) : Dag n k `
- `take_gate` (theorem, :462)  
  `@[simp] theorem take_gate (D : Dag n s) (k : ℕ) (hk : k ≤ s) (i : Fin k) : (D.take k hk).gate i = D.gate ⟨i.1, by omega⟩ `
- `val_take` (theorem, :466) — A prefix computes the same values on its wires.  
  `theorem val_take (D : Dag n s) (k : ℕ) (hk : k ≤ s) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + k)) : (D.take k hk).val x w = D.val x ⟨w.1, by omega⟩ `
- `nil` (def, :477) — The empty dag.  
  `def nil : Dag n 0 `
- `val_nil` (theorem, :479)  
  `@[simp] theorem val_nil (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + 0)) : (nil : Dag n 0).val x w = x w `
- `snoc` (def, :483) — Append one gate.  
  `def snoc (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) : Dag n (s + 1) `
- `init` (def, :488) — All gates but the last.  
  `def init (D : Dag n (s + 1)) : Dag n s `
- `last` (def, :491) — The last gate.  
  `def last (D : Dag n (s + 1)) : KGate (n + s) `
- `snoc_gate_castSucc` (theorem, :493)  
  `@[simp] theorem snoc_gate_castSucc (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) (i : Fin s) : (D.snoc g).gate i.castSucc = D.gate i `
- `snoc_gate_last` (theorem, :497)  
  `@[simp] theorem snoc_gate_last (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) : (D.snoc g).gate (Fin.last s) = g `
- `init_snoc` (theorem, :501)  
  `@[simp] theorem init_snoc (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) : (D.snoc g).init = D `
- `last_snoc` (theorem, :505)  
  `@[simp] theorem last_snoc (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) : (D.snoc g).last = g `
- `snoc_init_last` (theorem, :508)  
  `@[simp] theorem snoc_init_last (D : Dag n (s + 1)) : D.init.snoc D.last = D `
- `val_snoc_castSucc` (theorem, :515) — The old wires of `snoc` keep their values.  
  `@[simp] theorem val_snoc_castSucc (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.snoc g).val x w.castSucc = D.val x w `
- `val_snoc_last` (theorem, :522) — The new wire of `snoc` is the new gate on the old values.  
  `@[simp] theorem val_snoc_last (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)) (x : Fin n → Bool) : (D.snoc g).val x (Fin.last (n + s)) = g.eval (D.val x) `
- `snocInduction` (theorem, :529) — **Snoc induction** on dags.  
  `theorem snocInduction {motive : ∀ s, Dag n s → Prop} (hnil : motive 0 nil) (hsnoc : ∀ s (D : Dag n s) (g : KGate (n + s)), motive s D → motive (s + 1) (D.snoc g)) : ∀ s (D : Dag n s), motive s D `
- `mapIdx` (def, :546) — Rewrite every gate in place (the rewrite may depend on the gate index).  
  `def mapIdx (D : Dag n s) (f : (i : Fin s) → KGate (n + i) → KGate (n + i)) : Dag n s `
- `mapIdx_gate` (theorem, :549)  
  `@[simp] theorem mapIdx_gate (D : Dag n s) (f : (i : Fin s) → KGate (n + i) → KGate (n + i)) (i : Fin s) : (D.mapIdx f).gate i = f i (D.gate i) `
- `constNot` (def, :555) — Every NOT gate becomes the constant `b`.  
  `def constNot (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) : Dag n s `
- `constNot_gate` (theorem, :557)  
  `@[simp] theorem constNot_gate (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) (i : Fin s) : (D.constNot b).gate i = (D.gate i).constNot b `
- `constNot_notFree` (theorem, :560)  
  `theorem constNot_notFree (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) : (D.constNot b).NotFree `
- `constNot_of_notFree` (theorem, :563)  
  `theorem constNot_of_notFree (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) (hD : D.NotFree) : D.constNot b = D `
- `constNot_mono` (theorem, :567)  
  `theorem constNot_mono (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) (hD : D.MonoOffNot) : (D.constNot b).Mono `
- `_root_.PEqualNP.Kernel.KGate.constNot_false_le` (theorem, :570)  
  `theorem _root_.PEqualNP.Kernel.KGate.constNot_false_le {k : ℕ} (g : KGate k) (hg : g.isNot = false → OpMono g.op) {v v' : Fin k → Bool} (h : v ≤ v') : (g.constNot false).eval v ≤ g.eval v' `
- `_root_.PEqualNP.Kernel.KGate.le_constNot_true` (theorem, :579)  
  `theorem _root_.PEqualNP.Kernel.KGate.le_constNot_true {k : ℕ} (g : KGate k) (hg : g.isNot = false → OpMono g.op) {v v' : Fin k → Bool} (h : v ≤ v') : g.eval v ≤ (g.constNot true).eval v' `
- `constNot_false_le` (theorem, :591) — **Sandwich** `C0 ≤ C ≤ C1`, for any number of NOT gates (the other gates monotone, any fan-in), in the monotone form: the lower world at a smaller input is belo  
  `theorem constNot_false_le (D : Dag n s) (hD : D.MonoOffNot) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : x ≤ y) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.constNot false).val x w ≤ D.val y w `
- `le_constNot_true` (theorem, :595)  
  `theorem le_constNot_true (D : Dag n s) (hD : D.MonoOffNot) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : x ≤ y) (w : Fin (n + s)) : D.val x w ≤ (D.constNot true).val y w `
- `sandwich` (theorem, :599)  
  `theorem sandwich (D : Dag n s) (hD : D.MonoOffNot) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.constNot false).val x w ≤ D.val x w ∧ D.val x w ≤ (D.constNot true).val x w `
- `val_constNot_of_eq` (theorem, :604) — If every NOT gate of `D` has value `b` at `x`, then `D` and `D.constNot b` agree at `x`.  
  `theorem val_constNot_of_eq (D : Dag n s) (b : Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) (h : ∀ i, (D.gate i).isNot = true → D.val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = b) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.constNot b).val x w = D.val x w `
- `val_eq_constNot_of_atMostOne` (theorem, :622) — **One NOT: two worlds.** With at most one NOT gate, at every input the circuit coincides (on all wires) with the lower world `constNot false` or with the upper   
  `theorem val_eq_constNot_of_atMostOne (D : Dag n s) (h1 : ∀ i j, (D.gate i).isNot = true → (D.gate j).isNot = true → i = j) (x : Fin n → Bool) : (∀ w, (D.constNot false).val x w = D.val x w) ∨ (∀ w, (D.constNot true).val `
- `Closed` (def, :644) — `K` (a set of wires) is closed under reads: a gate in `K` reads only wires in `K`.  
  `def Closed (D : Dag n s) (K : Fin (n + s) → Prop) : Prop `
- `mask` (def, :648) — Gates outside `K` become the constant `false`; no reindexing.  
  `def mask (D : Dag n s) (K : Fin (n + s) → Prop) [DecidablePred K] : Dag n s `
- `mask_gate` (theorem, :651)  
  `theorem mask_gate (D : Dag n s) (K : Fin (n + s) → Prop) [DecidablePred K] (i : Fin s) : (D.mask K).gate i = if K (Fin.natAdd n i) then D.gate i else KGate.const false `
- `val_mask` (theorem, :655) — Masking with a closed set does not change the wires in the set.  
  `theorem val_mask (D : Dag n s) (K : Fin (n + s) → Prop) [DecidablePred K] (hK : D.Closed K) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : K w) : (D.mask K).val x w = D.val x w `
- `val_mask_out` (theorem, :665) — A masked-away gate is `false`.  
  `theorem val_mask_out (D : Dag n s) (K : Fin (n + s) → Prop) [DecidablePred K] (x : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) (hi : ¬ K (Fin.natAdd n i)) : (D.mask K).val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = false `
- `mask_true` (theorem, :670)  
  `theorem mask_true (D : Dag n s) : D.mask (fun _ => True) = D `
- `Closed.val_congr` (theorem, :674) — The wires of a closed set depend only on the inputs in the set.  
  `theorem Closed.val_congr {D : Dag n s} {K : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (hK : D.Closed K) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : ∀ j : Fin n, K (Fin.castAdd s j) → x j = y j) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : K w) : D.val x w = D.val y w `
- `Reads` (def, :681) — Wire `u` reads wire `v` directly.  
  `def Reads (D : Dag n s) (u v : Fin (n + s)) : Prop `
- `cone` (def, :685) — The cone of a set `O` of wires: everything reachable from `O` along reads.  
  `def cone (D : Dag n s) (O : Fin (n + s) → Prop) (w : Fin (n + s)) : Prop `
- `subset_cone` (theorem, :688)  
  `theorem subset_cone (D : Dag n s) (O : Fin (n + s) → Prop) {o : Fin (n + s)} (ho : O o) : D.cone O o `
- `cone_closed` (theorem, :691)  
  `theorem cone_closed (D : Dag n s) (O : Fin (n + s) → Prop) : D.Closed (D.cone O) `
- `cone_subset` (theorem, :696) — The cone is the least closed set containing `O`.  
  `theorem cone_subset (D : Dag n s) {O K : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (hK : D.Closed K) (hO : ∀ o, O o → K o) : ∀ w, D.cone O w → K w `
- `cone_mono` (theorem, :705)  
  `theorem cone_mono (D : Dag n s) {O O' : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (h : ∀ o, O o → O' o) : ∀ w, D.cone O w → D.cone O' w `
- `cone_le` (theorem, :710) — A wire in a cone is not above every output: cones only go down.  
  `theorem cone_le (D : Dag n s) {O : Fin (n + s) → Prop} {w : Fin (n + s)} (hw : D.cone O w) : ∃ o, O o ∧ w ≤ o `
- `val_mask_cone` (theorem, :721) — Masking with the cone of the outputs keeps the outputs (and their whole cone).  
  `theorem val_mask_cone (D : Dag n s) (O : Fin (n + s) → Prop) [DecidablePred (D.cone O)] (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : D.cone O w) : (D.mask (D.cone O)).val x w = D.val x w `
- `val_congr_cone` (theorem, :727) — The cone of the outputs depends only on the inputs in the cone.  
  `theorem val_congr_cone (D : Dag n s) (O : Fin (n + s) → Prop) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : ∀ j : Fin n, D.cone O (Fin.castAdd s j) → x j = y j) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : D.cone O w) : D.val x w = D.val y w `
- `wireρ` (def, :736) — A partial assignment of the inputs, seen as a partial assignment of the wires below `k` gates (gate wires are never fixed).  
  `def wireρ (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (k : ℕ) (a : Fin (n + k)) : Option Bool `
- `override` (def, :740) — The input `x` with the fixed coordinates overwritten.  
  `def override (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) (j : Fin n) : Bool `
- `override_of_none` (theorem, :743)  
  `theorem override_of_none {ρ : Fin n → Option Bool} {j : Fin n} (h : ρ j = none) (x : Fin n → Bool) : override ρ x j = x j `
- `override_of_some` (theorem, :746)  
  `theorem override_of_some {ρ : Fin n → Option Bool} {j : Fin n} {c : Bool} (h : ρ j = some c) (x : Fin n → Bool) : override ρ x j = c `
- `restrict` (def, :752) — **Restriction.** Fix the inputs `j` with `ρ j = some c` to `c`: every gate is simplified in place (`KGate.subst`), so the result has the same number of gates, t  
  `def restrict (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) : Dag n s `
- `restrict_gate` (theorem, :755)  
  `@[simp] theorem restrict_gate (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (i : Fin s) : (D.restrict ρ).gate i = (D.gate i).subst (wireρ ρ i) `
- `restrict_reads` (theorem, :759) — No gate of a restricted dag reads a fixed input.  
  `theorem restrict_reads (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (i : Fin s) (a : Fin (n + i)) (ha : a ∈ ((D.restrict ρ).gate i).reads) (j : Fin n) (hj : lift i a = Fin.castAdd s j) : ρ j = none `
- `restrict_notFree` (theorem, :768)  
  `theorem restrict_notFree (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (hD : D.NotFree) : (D.restrict ρ).NotFree `
- `restrict_mono` (theorem, :771)  
  `theorem restrict_mono (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (hD : D.Mono) : (D.restrict ρ).Mono `
- `val_restrict` (theorem, :775) — On inputs that respect the assignment, restriction changes no wire.  
  `theorem val_restrict (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) (hx : ∀ j c, ρ j = some c → x j = c) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.restrict ρ).val x w = D.val x w `
- `val_restrict_congr` (theorem, :794) — A restricted dag ignores the fixed inputs (on every wire except those fixed inputs).  
  `theorem val_restrict_congr (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : ∀ j, ρ j = none → x j = y j) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : ∀ j, w = Fin.castAdd s j → ρ j = none) : (D.restrict ρ).val x w = (D.res`
- `val_restrict_override` (theorem, :806) — **Restriction computes the function on the subcube**: at every unfixed wire, the restricted dag on `x` is the original dag on `x` with the fixed inputs overwrit  
  `theorem val_restrict_override (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) (w : Fin (n + s)) (hw : ∀ j, w = Fin.castAdd s j → ρ j = none) : (D.restrict ρ).val x w = D.val (override ρ x) w `
- `val_restrict_gate` (theorem, :812)  
  `theorem val_restrict_gate (D : Dag n s) (ρ : Fin n → Option Bool) (x : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) : (D.restrict ρ).val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = D.val (override ρ x) (Fin.natAdd n i) `
- `geρ` (def, :820) — The assignment fixing to `true` the inputs where `z` is `true`.  
  `def geρ (z : Fin n → Bool) (j : Fin n) : Option Bool `
- `restrictGE` (def, :823) — Restriction to the subcube `{x | z ≤ x}`.  
  `def restrictGE (D : Dag n s) (z : Fin n → Bool) : Dag n s `
- `override_geρ` (theorem, :825)  
  `theorem override_geρ (z x : Fin n → Bool) : override (geρ z) x = x ⊔ z `
- `val_restrictGE` (theorem, :830) — On the subcube, `restrictGE` changes no wire.  
  `theorem val_restrictGE (D : Dag n s) (z x : Fin n → Bool) (hzx : z ≤ x) (w : Fin (n + s)) : (D.restrictGE z).val x w = D.val x w `
- `val_restrictGE_gate` (theorem, :842) — Everywhere, a gate of `restrictGE z` at `x` is the original gate at `x ⊔ z`.  
  `theorem val_restrictGE_gate (D : Dag n s) (z x : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) : (D.restrictGE z).val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = D.val (x ⊔ z) (Fin.natAdd n i) `
- `val_restrictGE_congr` (theorem, :847) — Gates of `restrictGE z` do not look at the inputs fixed by `z`.  
  `theorem val_restrictGE_congr (D : Dag n s) (z : Fin n → Bool) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : ∀ j, z j = false → x j = y j) (i : Fin s) : (D.restrictGE z).val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = (D.restrictGE z).val y (Fin.natAdd n i) `
- `restrictGE_notFree` (theorem, :857)  
  `theorem restrictGE_notFree (D : Dag n s) (z : Fin n → Bool) (hD : D.NotFree) : (D.restrictGE z).NotFree `
- `restrictGE_mono` (theorem, :860)  
  `theorem restrictGE_mono (D : Dag n s) (z : Fin n → Bool) (hD : D.Mono) : (D.restrictGE z).Mono `
- `orOf` (def, :866) — The OR of the inputs in `S`.  
  `def orOf (S : Finset (Fin n)) (x : Fin n → Bool) : Bool `
- `orOf_mono` (theorem, :868)  
  `theorem orOf_mono (S : Finset (Fin n)) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : x ≤ y) : orOf S x ≤ orOf S y `
- `ComputesOn` (def, :876) — Wire `w` computes `f` on the set `A` of inputs.  
  `def ComputesOn (D : Dag n s) (A : (Fin n → Bool) → Prop) (w : Fin (n + s)) (f : (Fin n → Bool) → Bool) : Prop `
- `computesOn_ge_iff` (theorem, :881) — **A gate on the subcube `{x ≥ z}`** computes `f` there iff the same gate of `restrictGE z` computes `x ↦ f (x ⊔ z)` everywhere.  
  `theorem computesOn_ge_iff (D : Dag n s) (z : Fin n → Bool) (i : Fin s) (f : (Fin n → Bool) → Bool) : D.ComputesOn (fun x => z ≤ x) (Fin.natAdd n i) f ↔ ∀ x, (D.restrictGE z).val x (Fin.natAdd n i) = f (x ⊔ z) `
- `Closed.val_congr₂` (theorem, :895) — If the wires in a set agree off `Bad`-free cones: two dags with the same gates on a closed set `K` agree on `K` (the two-circuit form of `Closed.val_congr`).  
  `theorem Closed.val_congr₂ {D D' : Dag n s} {K : Fin (n + s) → Prop} (hK : D.Closed K) (hDD' : ∀ i : Fin s, K (Fin.natAdd n i) → D.gate i = D'.gate i) {x y : Fin n → Bool} (hxy : ∀ j : Fin n, K (Fin.castAdd s j) → x j = y`
- `SnocLaw` (def, :913) — The snoc law of a list evaluator `V` on `n` inputs with gate semantics `ev`.

- `LGate` (abbrev, :27) — W951's list gates.

- `rd` (def, :39) — W951's read: the value of wire `j`, `false` if it is not below the gate.
